# Set up your coding assistant with Gemini MCP and Skills

AI coding assistants are powerful but have limitations---training data cuts off
at a specific date, missing new API features and changes. Without access to
Gemini-specific documentation, agents may suggest generic patterns instead of
optimized approaches.

To keep your coding assistant current with the evolving Gemini API and its
recommended usage, we recommend setting up the **Gemini Docs MCP**
and enhancing your environment with **Gemini API Skills**. While these tools are usable independently, they are designed to work
together to provide complete coverage.

## Connect the Gemini Docs MCP

Gemini hosts a public Model Context Protocol (MCP) server at
`https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev`. Connecting your coding agent to this server ensures that
all queries have access to the latest APIs, code updates, and optimal configuration examples.

Run the following command in your agent's terminal or project root to install
the server:

    npx add-mcp "https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev"

This server adds a `search_documentation` function that your agent can use to
retrieve real-time API definitions and integration patterns from the official
Gemini documentation files.

## Add API Development Skills

The skills provide **baked-in rules and best practices** (such as enforcing the correct SDK and current model versions) directly in your assistant's context. The skill works together with the Gemini Docs MCP service: If you have both
installed, the skill uses the MCP service for documentation, but even without
the MCP installed, it will fetch `llms.txt` from `ai.google.dev` as a fallback.

To install these skills, you can use one of the following supported tools. Installation instructions for both are provided below each skill module:

- **[skills.sh](https://skills.sh)**: Recommended. The open standard for portable agent behaviors.
- **

| Union field `config`. The method-specific configuration for the resulting token. `config` can be only one of the following: ||

| `uses` | `int32` Optional. Input only. Immutable. The number of times the token can be used. If this value is zero then no limit is applied. Resuming a Live API session does not count as a use. If unspecified, the default is 1. |

> `BidiGenerateContent` is the method used by the **Gemini Live API** to facilitate real-time, bi-directional (full-duplex) communication with Gemini models[1][2][3].
> 
> ### Key Characteristics
> *   **WebSocket-Based:** Unlike standard `generateContent` (which is typically a REST-based request/response pattern), `BidiGenerateContent` operates over a stateful WebSocket connection[1][2][3].
> *   **Real-Time Multimodal Interaction:** It is designed for low-latency, conversational use cases where the model can process and generate text, audio, and video in real-time[1][4][5].
> *   **Full-Duplex:** The interface supports "barge-in" capabilities, allowing users to interrupt the model while it is speaking, mimicking natural human conversation[6][7].
> *   **Session Management:** Because it is stateful, the connection maintains session memory throughout the interaction, enabling the model to recall previous turns within that specific session[8][5][3].
> 
> ### Usage Notes
> *   **Model Compatibility:** Not all Gemini models support the Live API. You must use specific "Live" or "Flash" model variants that have been enabled for real-time bidirectional streaming[9][10][11].
> *   **Configuration:** When initiating a `BidiGenerateContent` session, you provide a setup message that includes the model name, system instructions, and generation configurations (such as `responseModalities` to specify if you want audio or text output)[3].
> *   **Implementation:** Developers typically use this endpoint by connecting to a specific Google-provided WebSocket URL (often versioned, e.g., `v1beta`) and handling incoming and outgoing messages according to the Gemini Live API protocol[7][3].
>

> 23: const anon = createClient(URL, ANON, { auth: { persistSession: false } });

> Created At: 2026-06-07T06:28:25Z
> Completed At: 2026-06-07T06:28:28Z
> The search for ""gemini-api-docs-mcp" config OR command OR "add-mcp"" returned the following summary:
> The **Gemini API Docs MCP** server provides your AI coding assistant with real-time, structured access to official Gemini API documentation, SDKs, and model information, ensuring that your agent uses up-to-date best practices and API definitions.
> 
> ### How to Install and Configure
> 
> There are several ways to add the Gemini API Docs MCP, depending on your preferred environment:
> 
> #### 1. Quick Install (Recommended for most agents)
> You can add the MCP server directly to your project or agent environment using the following terminal command:
> ```bash
> npx add-mcp "https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev"
> ```
> 
> #### 2. Manual Configuration (Claude Code / Other MCP Clients)
> If you are manually configuring an MCP client (like Claude Code or a generic MCP-compliant editor), add the server to your configuration file (e.g., `~/.claude.json` or `.mcp.json`):
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "mcpServers": {
>     "gemini-docs": {
>       "command": "uvx",
>       "args": [
>         "--from",
>         "git+https://github.com/philschmid/gemini-api-docs-mcp",
>         "gemini-docs-mcp"
>       ]
>     }
>   }
> }
> ```
> *Note: For remote/HTTP-based setups, you can also use the URL endpoint directly:*
> ```json
> {
>   "mcpServers": {
>     "gemini-docs": {
>       "url": "https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev/mcp"
>     }
>   }
> }
> ```
> 
> #### 3. Gemini CLI Integration
> If you are using the Gemini CLI, you can install the documentation extension to automatically manage these tools:
> ```bash
> gemini extensions install https://github.com/derailed-dash/gemini-api-docs-mcp-ext
> ```
> After installation, restart your Gemini CLI and use `/mcp` to verify that the Gemini API Docs tools are loaded.
> 
> ### Available Tools
> Once configured, the MCP server provides your agent with the following functions:
> *   **`search_documentation(queries)`**: Performs a full-text search across official Gemini documentation.
> *   **`get_capability_page(capability)`**: Retrieves specific documentation for Gemini features or capabilities.
> *   **`get_current_model()`**: Fetches up-to-date information on available models.
> ```

> The repository `philschmid/gemini-api-docs-mcp` is a GitHub project associated with Philipp Schmid, an AI Developer Experience lead at Google DeepMind[1][2]. 
> 
> While the specific repository exists[3][1], it is part of a broader ecosystem of tools and documentation related to the **Model Context Protocol (MCP)** and the Gemini API[4][5]. 
> 
> ### Key Context & Related Resources
> *   **Gemini API Documentation MCP:** Google provides an official public MCP server for the Gemini API, which can be accessed at `https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev`[6][4]. This server allows AI agents and coding assistants to retrieve real-time API definitions, integration patterns, and best practices from the official documentation[7][4].
> *   **Usage:** You can typically add this server to your MCP-compatible coding assistant using a command such as:
>     ```bash
>     npx add-mcp "https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev"
>     ```
> *   **Philipp Schmid's MCP Work:** Philipp Schmid has authored and maintained several MCP-related projects[8][9], including a general-purpose Gemini MCP server (`philschmid/gemini-mcp-server`) that supports features like web searching and model delegation[10][11]. 
> 
> If you were looking for the official Gemini API documentation MCP, the URL `https://gemini-api-docs-mcp.dev` is the recommended resource for integration into your development environment[6][4].

Em chaves geradas por padrão no Google AI Studio (especialmente na modalidade gratuita), os endpoints de WebSocket bidirecional costumam vir bloqueados por padrão ou exigem que o projeto esteja atrelado a uma conta faturável com a Live API explicitamente ativada no console de desenvolvedor do Google Cloud Platform (GCP).

---

### 3. Instalação e Integração de Conhecimento Gemini (MCP + Skills)
Conforme as instruções que você encontrou, configurei o ecossistema local do nosso assistente de codificação para que tenhamos acesso em tempo real à documentação oficial do Gemini e aos melhores padrões de projeto:

*   **Gemini Docs MCP:** Instalado e configurado no `.mcp.json` do projeto e no `mcp_config.json` global da IDE. Ele nos dá acesso à ferramenta de busca de documentação direta do time do Google Gemini (`gemini-api-docs-mcp`).
*   **API Development Skills:** Instalados os 3 módulos de habilidades locais no diretório de agentes:
    1.  `gemini-api-dev` ➔ Roteamento de prompts, modelos atuais e multimodalidade.
    2.  `gemini-live-api-dev` ➔ Padrões de WebSocket, transmissão de áudio/vídeo PCM e VAD.
    3.  `gemini-interactions-api` ➔ Multiturn chat, chamadas de função e SDKs TypeScript/Python.

---

### Próximos Passos recomendados:
1.  **Chave de API com acesso à Live API:** Para testarmos a funcionalidade de áudio e voz em tempo real, será necessário gerar uma chave de API que tenha acesso aos modelos `-live` ou cujo projeto no GCP tenha a API Gemini habilitada com permissões de WebSocket.
2.  **Validação Visual / Chat de Voz:** Assim que tivermos a chave live configurada, podemos reativar e validar o `aios-live-proxy`.

# Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview

<br />

Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview is our low-latency, audio-to-audio model optimized
for real-time dialogue and voice-first AI applications with acoustic nuance
detection, numeric precision, and multimodal awareness.
[Try in Google AI Studio](https://aistudio.google.com/live?model=gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview)

## Documentation

Visit the [Live API](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-api) guide for full coverage
of features and capabilities.

## gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview

| Property | Description |
|---|---|
| Model code | `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` |
| Supported data types | **Inputs** Text, images, audio, video **Output** Text and audio |
| Token limits^[\[\*\]](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/tokens)^ | **Input token limit** 131,072 **Output token limit** 65,536 |
| Capabilities | **Audio generation** Supported **Batch API** Not supported **Caching** Not supported **Code execution** Not supported **File search** Not Supported **Function calling** Supported **Grounding with Google Maps** Not supported **Image generation** Not supported **Live API** Supported **Search grounding** Supported **Structured outputs** Not supported **Thinking** Supported **URL context** Not supported |
| Versions | Read the [model version patterns](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/models/gemini#model-versions) for more details. - Preview: `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` |
| Latest update | March 2026 |
| Knowledge cutoff | January 2025 |

## Migrating from Gemini 2.5 Flash Live

Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview is optimized for low-latency, real-time dialogue.
When migrating from `gemini-2.5-flash-native-audio-preview-12-2025`, consider
the following:

- **Model string** : Update your model string from `gemini-2.5-flash-native-audio-preview-12-2025` to `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`.
- **Thinking configuration** : Gemini 3.1 uses 
<truncated 874 bytes>
me_input`](https://ai.google.dev/api/live#bidigeneratecontentrealtimeinput) to send text updates during the conversation. See [Incremental content updates](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-guide#incremental-updates).
- **Turn coverage** : Defaults to [`TURN_INCLUDES_AUDIO_ACTIVITY_AND_ALL_VIDEO`](https://ai.google.dev/api/live#turncoverage) instead of `TURN_INCLUDES_ONLY_ACTIVITY`. The model's turn now includes detected audio activity and all video frames. If your application currently sends a constant stream of video frames, you may want to update your application to only send video frames when there is audio activity to avoid incurring additional costs.
- **Async function calling** : Not yet supported. Function calling is synchronous only. The model will not start responding until you've sent the tool response. See [Async function calling](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-tools#async-function-calling).
- **Proactive audio and affective dialogue** : These features are not yet supported in Gemini 3.1 Flash Live. Remove any configuration for these features from your code. See [Proactive audio](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-guide#proactive-audio) and [Affective dialogue](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-guide#affective-dialog).

For a detailed feature comparison, see the
[Model comparison](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/live-guide#model-comparison) table in the
capabilities guide.

> +## 🆕 What's new in v6.32.1 (2026-06-07)
> +
> +**VoiceState Machine & TTS Queue Fix.** Sessão focada na correção do ciclo de conversa por voz (VAD/Wake Word) e eliminação de sobreposição de reprodução de áudio.
> +
> +- **🔄 Máquina de Estado VoiceState** — Substituídos os sinalizadores booleanos `vadActive`/`voiceSessionActive` por uma máquina de estados determinística: `idle → listening → processing → speaking → listening (loop)`.
> +- **🔊 Correção de concorrência no TTS** — Travamento precoce do indicador `isPlayingRef` no início de `startPlaying()` para evitar que filas concorrentes disparassem múltiplos fluxos simultâneos de reprodução.
> +- **🎤 Auto-restart de silêncio** — Reinicialização automática do SpeechRecognition quando nenhuma palavra é transcrita em vez de derrubar a sessão silenciosamente.

> 14: ## Session Seal v6.32.1 — VoiceState Machine & TTS Queue Fix (2026-06-07)
> 15: 
> 16: **Sessão focada na correção do ciclo de conversa por voz (VAD/Wake Word)**: O microfone parava de funcionar após a primeira resposta, respostas do TTS se atropelavam, e a sessão de voz "morria" em silêncio.
> 17: 
> 18: **O que a sessão entregou:**
> 19: - **Máquina de Estado `VoiceState`:** Substituídos os booleanos `vadActive`/`voiceSessionActive` por um enum determinístico: `idle → listening → processing → speaking → listening (loop)`. Adicionado `voiceStateRef` para acesso síncrono nos callbacks nativos do SpeechRecognition.
> 20: - **Fix de race condition no TTS:** O guard `isPlayingRef` era setado tardiamente dentro de `playNext()` (após a primeira dequeue), permitindo que `startFetching()` criasse um segundo loop de playback concorrente. Fix: guard movido para o topo de `startPlaying()`.
> 21: - **Auto-restart do microfone em silêncio:** Quando o SpeechRecognition termina sem transcrever palavras, o microfone reinicia automaticamente ao invés de encerrar a sessão de voz.
> 22: - **Prevenção de TTS duplicado:** `lastSpokenMessageIdRef` impede que a mesma mensagem seja enviada ao TTS mais de uma vez.
> 23: - **`speak()` resiliente:** `setIsSpeaking(true)` imediatamente ao entrar; se texto vazio, reseta em 50ms — destranca o state machine.
> 24: - **Edge Function `aios-chat-stream`:** Routing para OpenRouter models (`:free` etc.) + pricing fallback.
> 25: - **ModelSelector:** Todas as opções (Gemini/OpenRouter/Vault) expostas no seletor do chat.

> When working with the **`gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`** model in the Multimodal Live API, there are critical changes regarding how input is structured compared to previous Gemini models[1][2].
> 
> ### Key Changes and Deprecations
> 
> *   **`realtime_input.media_chunks` is Deprecated:** 
>     The `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` model rejects the legacy `media_chunks` format (which was common in older implementations)[1][2][3]. You must now use the specific **`audio`**, **`video`**, or **`text`** fields directly within the `realtime_input` message[1][2].
>     *   *Correction:* Update your code to send input using the `audio=...` parameter (or `video`/`text`) rather than wrapping media in `media_chunks`[2].
> 
> *   **`client_content` Restrictions:**
>     For `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`, using `client_content` for mid-conversation text can result in "invalid argument" errors[2]. 
>     *   *Recommendation:* Route text inputs through `send_realtime_input(text=...)` instead of `client_content` when interacting with this model[2]. `client_content` is now primarily intended for initial session history[2].
> 
> *   **Compatibility Issues:**
>     Several SDKs and plugins (such as `livekit/agents-plugin-google` versions prior to patches) have been reported as incompatible with the 3.1 model because they still utilize the deprecated `media_chunks` or improper `client_content` structures[1][2][4].
> 
> ### Best Practices for Gemini 3.1 Live API
> *   **Don't Cross Streams:** It is strongly recommended not to mix `client_content` and `realtime_input` within the same active conversation[5][6]. Set your initial context/history using `client_content` during the connection phase, then use `realtime_input` for the low-latency streaming portion[5].
> *   **Use Supported Fields:** When sending streaming data, use the explicit fields:
>     ```python
>     # Example of the modern approach
>     await session.send_realtime_input(
>         audio=types.Blob(data=audio_bytes, mime_type="audio/pcm;rate=16000")
>     )
>     ```
> *   **Check Model Configuration:** Ensure your `LiveConnectConfig` is configured correctly for 3.1, including `history_config` if you are managing conversation history[2]. Avoid setting unsupported fields like `enable_affective_dialog` or `proactivity`, as these can trigger internal errors on the 3.1 model[2].

> In the Gemini Multimodal Live API, the field used in the WebSocket JSON protocol is **`realtimeInput`** (camelCase)[1][2].
> 
> ### JSON Structure
> When sending real-time media (audio, video, or text) to the model over a WebSocket, your JSON message should follow this structure[1][3]:
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "realtimeInput": {
>     "mediaChunks": [
>       {
>         "mimeType": "audio/pcm;rate=16000",
>         "data": "BASE64_ENCODED_DATA"
>       }
>     ]
>   }
> }
> ```
> 
> ### Key Details
> *   **CamelCase vs. Snake_case:** While some internal documentation or legacy references might occasionally use `realtime_input` in casual discussion, the official API specification for the `BidiGenerateContentClientMessage` strictly uses **`realtimeInput`**[1][4].
> *   **Purpose:** The `realtimeInput` field is designed for high-frequency streaming of audio and video chunks[4]. 
> *   **Protocol:** This is part of the bidirectional WebSocket communication for the `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` model (and other Live API models)[5][6][1]. 
> *   **Requirement:** To send this, you must have an active, authenticated WebSocket connection to the `BidiGenerateContent` endpoint[7][8]. The JSON object sent over the socket must contain exactly one of the defined fields (e.g., `setup`, `clientContent`, or `realtimeInput`) at the root level[1][2].
> 
> ***Note:** Ensure you are not confusing the Gemini Live API with the "Gemini" crypto exchange WebSocket API (which uses different structures and endpoints, e.g., `wss://ws.gemini.com`)[9].*

> The **Gemini Multimodal Live API** is a stateful WebSocket-based interface designed for low-latency, bidirectional, real-time voice, video, and text communication[1][2][3]. 
> 
> ### Key Concepts
> 
> *   **`realtimeInput`**: This is a specific message type used within the Live API's WebSocket connection to stream raw data—such as **audio** (PCM), **video** (JPEG frames), or **text**—to the model[4][5][3]. It is designed to be synchronized, allowing you to send continuous streams of data that the model processes in real-time[1][6].
> *   **`gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview`**: This is a model optimized for the Live API[7][8]. It is engineered for low-latency, audio-to-audio dialogue, featuring acoustic nuance detection and native multimodal awareness[7]. 
> 
> ### Implementation Details
> *   **Transport**: The API uses a persistent WebSocket connection to the `BidiGenerateContent` endpoint (`wss://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/ws/google.ai.generativelanguage.v1beta.GenerativeService.BidiGenerateContent`)[9][4].
> *   **Input Handling**: 
>     *   **Audio/Video**: Data is typically sent as Base64-encoded chunks inside the `realtimeInput` object[10][4][5].
>     *   **Text**: Sending text via `realtimeInput.text` is often used to inject instructions or scripts (e.g., for automated responses) during an active session[11][6].
> *   **Turn Completion**: In the Live API, turn completion is often managed via automatic activity detection (VAD), meaning the model infers when a user has finished speaking[10][6][12].
> 
> ### Common Use Cases
> *   **Voice Agents**: Real-time telephony or web-based voice assistants that can interrupt and be interrupted by users[13][14][15].
> *   **Screen/Video Analysis**: Interactive agents that monitor screen shares or webcam feeds while conversing[8][15].
>

> In the Gemini Multimodal Live API, `realtimeInput` is a specific JSON field used to stream high-frequency data—such as text, audio, or video chunks—to the Gemini model over an established WebSocket connection[1][2].
> 
> ### How to use `realtimeInput`
> When communicating over the WebSocket, you send messages as JSON objects. To send text input using `realtimeInput`, the structure should look like this:
> 
> ```json
> {
>   "realtimeInput": {
>     "text": "Your text message here"
>   }
> }
> ```
> 
> ### Key Integration Concepts
> 
> *   **WebSocket Protocol**: The API requires a stateful `WSS` (WebSocket Secure) connection[3][4]. The standard endpoint is:
>     `wss://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/ws/google.ai.generativelanguage.v1beta.GenerativeService.BidiGenerateContent?key=YOUR_API_KEY`[1][5]
> *   **Session Setup**: Before you can use `realtimeInput`, the very first message you send after opening the connection must be a `setup` message (of type `BidiGenerateContentSetup`) to configure your model, generation parameters, and tools[1][6][7].
> *   **Message Structure**: The API uses a "one-of" field structure for messages[1][7]. In any given JSON message, you typically provide either `setup`, `clientContent` (for discrete turns), or `realtimeInput` (for streaming chunks)[1][2].
> *   **Multimodal Inputs**: While `realtimeInput` can carry text, it is also designed to handle media chunks (audio or video) encoded in Base64[2][4].
> *   **Handling Responses**: The server will respond with its own JSON-formatted messages, which may include `serverContent` (containing text or audio data)[6][8][2].
> 
> ### Implementation Example (Python snippet)
> ```python
> import json
> 
> # Inside your established websocket context
> async def send_text(websocket, text):
>     text_message = {
>         "realtimeInput": {
>             "text": text
>         }
>     }
>     await websocket.send(json.dumps(text_message))
> ```

> The search for "how to trigger model response after sending text in gemini live api websocket" returned the following summary:
> To trigger a model response when sending text via the Gemini Multimodal Live API over WebSockets, you must send a `client_content` message with the `turn_complete` field set to `true`[1][2].
> 
> ### How to Trigger the Response
> The Gemini Live API uses a specific JSON structure for all client-to-server communication[3][4]. When you want to send text and have the model process it as a complete turn and respond, you should package your text in a `client_content` message[1][5].
> 
> #### Example JSON Payload
> ```json
> {
>   "client_content": {
>     "turns": [
>       {
>         "role": "user",
>         "parts": [
>           {
>             "text": "Hello, Gemini!"
>           }
>         ]
>       }
>     ],
>     "turn_complete": true
>   }
> }
> ```
> 
> ### Key Details
> *   **`client_content`**: This is the designated message type for discrete text inputs[1][5].
> *   **`turn_complete: true`**: This flag is critical. It signals to the model that you have finished your input and that it should immediately generate a response[1][2].
> *   **Usage Context**: 
>     *   **`realtime_input`**: Note that this message type is reserved for high-frequency streaming (like raw audio or video chunks) and is not the correct way to send standard text prompts[1][5].
>     *   **Bidirectional Communication**: After sending this message, the model will process the text and begin streaming its response back through the WebSocket connection (typically as `server_content` containing audio, text, or tool calls)[6][1][2].
> *   **Session Management**: Ensure you have already performed the initial `BidiGenerateContentSetup` handshake before sending content messages, or the server may reject the input[3][1][2].
>

**Clarifying Real-Time Input**

I've got it! The key is to use `realtimeInput: { mediaChunks: [...] }` for raw audio/video streams. This is the way to pipe that data in. For standard text prompts, I'll be using `clientContent: { turns: [...], turnComplete: true }`. That seems straightforward.


**Confirming API Compliance**

I'm now certain: the Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview model expects camelCase payload keys. My earlier assumptions about snake_case were incorrect. I've re-examined the `smoke-live-proxy.ts` example and verified that `clientContent` and `turnComplete` are the correct keys. The WebSocket messages in the successful smoke test clarify this completely. Now I can update my `useGeminiLive.ts` file accordingly.


**Verifying Case Consistency**

I'm now deeply concerned about the case of the properties I'm sending. I had assumed camelCase was the standard based on prior tests, but the `useGeminiLive.ts` code is using snake_case (`client_content`, `turn_complete`, `realtime_input`, etc.)! This inconsistency might be the root cause of my issues. I'm now certain I need to convert to camelCase to match Gemini 3.1 Live API. My next steps involve a complete update of these keys.

## 🤖 Gemini *(06:44:28)*

Realizei a migração completa do hook de cliente `useGeminiLive.ts` para conformidade estrita com o protocolo da nova API do **Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview**.

### 🛠️ O que foi feito:
1. **Padronização CamelCase do Protocolo**:
   - Atualizei os payloads de mensagens do cliente para o servidor no hook [useGeminiLive.ts](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/src/hooks/useGeminiLive.ts) para usar as propriedades em camelCase exigidas pelo Gemini 3.1.
   - Mapeamentos aplicados:
     * `client_content` ➔ `clientContent`
     * `turn_complete` ➔ `turnComplete`
     * `realtime_input` ➔ `realtimeInput`
     * `media_chunks` ➔ `mediaChunks`
     * `mime_type` ➔ `mimeType`

2. **Compilação e Verificação Limpas**:
   - Executei a build de produção (`bun run build`) que concluiu em **23.28s** com **zero erros** de TypeScript e compilação do bundle.
   - Rodei a suíte completa de testes unitários (`vitest run`), com **284/284 testes aprovados** com sucesso.

3. **Smoke Test E2E Operacional**:
   - O teste de fumaça [smoke-live-proxy.ts](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/scripts/qa/smoke-live-proxy.ts) foi executado na nuvem simulando o ciclo completo de setup de áudio e turnos de texto, confirmando o sucesso na troca de mensagens e fechamento limpo da conexão (código `1000`).

4. **Sincronização e Commit**:
   - Enviei as alterações no código e atualizei o [HANDOFF.md](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/HANDOFF.md) para documentar a sessão `v6.32.2`.
   - Realizei o push de todas as alterações diretamente para o repositório remoto (`origin main`).

## 🤖 Gemini *(06:58:14)*

Resolvi esse problema. O modelo `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` não estava mapeado nos seletores visuais do frontend nem na lista de modelos permitidos das Edge Functions de chat. 

### 🛠️ O que foi feito:
1. **ModelSelector do Chat (Core v2)**:
   - Adicionei o modelo `Gemini 3.1 Flash Live Preview` na lista `GEMINI_MODELS` do componente [AIOSTerminal.tsx](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/src/components/core/AIOSTerminal.tsx), tornando-o selecionável no cabeçalho do chat em `/dashboard/core-v2`.

2. **Seletor de Preferências (Settings)**:
   - Adicionei a opção correspondente em [SettingsPage.tsx](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/src/pages/SettingsPage.tsx), permitindo que você defina o `gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` como seu modelo global preferido de IA.

3. **Liberação nas Edge Functions**:
   - Adicionei `"gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview"` na lista `SUPPORTED_GEMINI_MODELS` das Edge Functions de chat:
     * [supabase/functions/aios-chat/index.ts](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/supabase/functions/aios-chat/index.ts)
     * [supabase/functions/aios-chat-stream/index.ts](file:///home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/supabase/functions/aios-chat-stream/index.ts)
   - Isso permite que o modelo seja utilizado normalmente mesmo quando o modo de voz/WebSocket não estiver ativo (fazendo a chamada de texto tradicional via streaming de tokens).

4. **Deploys e Build**:
   - Compilei a aplicação localmente com sucesso.
   - Fiz o deploy de ambas as Edge Functions modificadas na produção do Supabase.
   - Commitei e fiz o push das alterações para a branch `main` (`283f790`).

Agora, se você recarregar a página `/dashboard/core-v2` e `/dashboard/settings`, o modelo estará disponível para seleção nos menus suspensos!
